# Firmware temperature offsets

For valid ambient readings, **Celsius = network `TemSen` − client offset**. This client correction is separate from removing a bias inside the firmware. Reported firmware `version` remains metadata; its major number does not select the encoding.

The expanded inventory covers **318 loose files, 279 distinct SHA-256 payloads**, including 250 payloads beyond the original 29-image investigation. It includes native firmware, application/filesystem packages, and non-firmware responses saved with firmware extensions. Duplicate bytes are one inventory row, not another build. Full hashes, download aliases, recovered identities, and CPU evidence are in [HIDS.md](docs/HIDS.md#downloaded-payload-inventory).

## Evidence levels

- **Confirmed:** calibration and network conversion were established inside the inspected image.
- **Schema-supported:** the firmware forwards UART byte 46 unchanged. Offset 40 follows the independently measured [Gree UART temperature schema](https://github.com/bekmansurov/gree-hvac-protocol#byte-46-indoor-temperature), where `62 − 40 = 22°C`. Physical calibration was not independently established inside each such image.
- **Runtime-dependent:** one image selects different encodings using runtime configuration; no single metadata-only offset was established.
- **N/A:** the inspected status schema has no actual `TemSen` decoder. This does **not** mean offset zero.
- **Unresolved:** a complete sensor-to-response path or its calibration could not be established; the row states the missing evidence. This is neither offset zero nor proof that the field is unsupported.
- **Not firmware:** the downloaded bytes are text/artwork rather than an executable firmware payload. No temperature offset or CPU is assigned.

Versions below use embedded/generated identities where recoverable, rather than trusting download filenames.

## Inspected builds

All 279 payloads are accounted for in the [per-payload findings](#per-payload-findings), with exact SHA links, data-flow evidence, executed checks, and blockers. A numeric result is scoped to the inspected implementation and sensor path, not every device sharing a code or MID.

| Finding | Distinct downloaded payloads |
| --- | ---: |
| Offset 0, internally confirmed | 12 |
| Offset 40, internally confirmed | 6 |
| Offset 40, UART-schema-supported | 67 |
| Runtime-dependent 0 or 40 | 5 |
| N/A: no local TemSen decoder | 111 |
| Unresolved: no assigned offset | 74 |
| Not firmware | 4 |

Two of the 67 schema-supported results apply only to selected gateway UART plugins. They are not gateway-wide defaults. The unresolved set includes encrypted/protected code, differential updates without their base, unsupported vendor instructions, missing attached-controller contracts, incomplete response paths, and shared caches without a measured-temperature producer.

## Trait selection

`greeclimate/device_profile.py` contains **61 firmware-code fallbacks**: seven offset-0 defaults, 49 offset-40 defaults, and five offset-40 bootstrap defaults with continuous re-learning. All 14 original defaults are preserved. Every production entry has `min_version=None`, deliberately applying its quirks across revisions and module tokens, including uninspected builds. This is a code-wide extrapolation of internally confirmed and standard-UART-supported findings, not new same-device calibration.

The frozen direct-submodule API is `DeviceProfile(firmware_code, temperature_offset, min_version=None, relearn_temperature_offset=False)` and `match_device_profile(hid)`. The former MID, module, literal-version and settings fields and two-argument matcher are removed without compatibility aliases. No package-root exports are added.

A complete, syntactically valid HID is required; missing, nonstring, malformed, bare-code and unknown-code inputs return no profile. Module and qualifier syntax is validated but does not constrain matching. For a matching code, the greatest eligible `min_version` wins, otherwise its no-minimum fallback; exact ties retain the first row. One whole profile is selected, never merged. Numeric dotted strings preserve components: `1.10 > 1.9`, `1.05 == 1.5`, `1.2 == 1.2.0`, and all-zero versions compare equally. Only the version parsed from the HID participates; raw HID and public version strings are preserved. Discovery `ver`, OTA `commProtVer`, MID and mutable `Device.version` do not select a version floor.

SN (`362001061381`), JD (`362001061383`), TMAC (`362001062557`), JDNEW (`362001064270`) and W04N (`362001064973`) enable re-learning across their entire code families. Their fallback 40 is only a bootstrap estimate. Earlier normalized SN V11, TMAC V10 and W04N V17 receive this same policy rather than version-specific rows: a first 25 learns offset 0; a first 45 provisionally displays 5°C.

The registry excludes route-only gateway code `362001068423`, fallback/default HID code `362001000968`, unconfirmed primary dispatch `362001061615`, cellular/initialization identity `362001063525`, and mislabelled download codes `362001061217`, `362001061646` and `362001064307`. RT13 V1.54 uses recovered primary code `362001065279`; the separate RT11 V1.44 evidence retains `362001065280`. W05K V1.05/V1.07 agree under `362001061234`, and both WB05UR V2.04 images agree under `362001068295`. Primary identities and exact SHA evidence remain in [HIDS.md](docs/HIDS.md), with route qualifications in [MIDS.md](docs/MIDS.md).

The mislabelled QCOM download does not justify a profile under `362001064307`. Debug/burn identifiers are not necessarily generated HIDs. API protocol-version fields and discovery versions are not substitutes for the reported firmware HID. No universal MID-to-offset mapping was established.

The three original confirmed zero-offset builds perform `clamp(raw − 40, 0, 50)` before JSON serialization; their paired setters add 40 back. A UART value of 65 therefore arrives as `TemSen=25`. The client must not subtract 40 again. The expanded per-payload results include additional normalized builds.

### Runtime-dependent and unsupported schemas

BL3332_JD V18's getter at `0x0802d368` selects client offset 40 for persisted/detected `startcloud_id == 9` (GREE), otherwise 0. Below encoded 40 it returns zero; above 90 it clamps. No public MID/HID/version field identifying the active selector was established. Its code-family profile enables continuous detection with a provisional offset-40 fallback, not a fixed encoding claim.

W05CA 1.39 returns generic zero for an unknown `TemSen` query, while 1.40 omits the unknown property. Neither response establishes a zero-degree sensor reading. Other N/A families expose different schemas, including `binStatus` and ATW/gateway-specific fields. This change does not implement those protocols or assign N/A builds a numeric trait. An initial zero remains unavailable until a zero-offset encoding is known.

### Version-specific counterexamples to code-only inference

These are comparisons of inspected binaries, not observations of a live device changing encoding during an OTA update. Earlier getters normalize unconditionally; later getters retain or remove the bias according to persisted `startcloud_id`. Version identifies the implementation, but cannot identify the encoding of an already cached response. The following table describes getter behavior; the [Wi-Fi follow-up](#wi-fi-protocol-indicators) traces the additional transport/cache boundary.

| Generated firmware code / module | Earlier inspected build | Later inspected build |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `362001061381` / `BL3332_SN` | [V11](docs/HIDS.md#sha-5b050eaef8e0b1e28e47c57cff60f642e9f73eb34ca46f69b3096ac3f6cb4d36): offset 0 | [V13](docs/HIDS.md#sha-c38c120c2f125dfc5e90917265860d0efb0bfa8089b8586fa2466b22fe17063b): offset 40 for selector 9, otherwise 0 |
| `362001062557` / `BL3332_TMAC` | [V10](docs/HIDS.md#sha-6da277aa8bc2135d14601e74d76692be9d96db0c87b6da422a62a32cd630d1d6): offset 0 | [V14](docs/HIDS.md#sha-7f8a0a059c8a434ac5caef6b42b78dcfdb112939d5d7027a7a0b743da50bb065): offset 40 for selector 9, otherwise 0 |
| `362001064973` / `W04N` | [V17](docs/HIDS.md#sha-b0b7f5d428858ca5e5ad29a8afcfaa0da4063fe36e98fae7c447047163112727): offset 0 | [V19](docs/HIDS.md#sha-0b01a0d0fd25651b81bf435e46428987a3b43b905b2ee6bdc0714c6030452d92): offset 40 for selector 9, otherwise 0 |

`362001064270+U-BL3332_JDNEWV12.bin` also uses this runtime branch. In these later getters, encoded input below 40 produces zero; input above 90 is clamped. The selector-9 path is not an unrestricted raw pass-through. The W04N V19 download requested under `362001061217` actually initializes code `362001064973`; it does not establish the behavior of the original issue's W04N V7.

### Gateway and incomplete-response limits

`362001068423` ZTS31A V3.09/V3.10 provide schema-supported offset 40 **only for the recovered routed UART plugins**, not a gateway-wide HID default. The proved routes are `13806 → lib13806.so`, `13807 → lib13807.so`, `11012/11002/10014 → lib11012.so`, `11000 → lib11000.so`, and `10f05 → lib10f05.so`. Other plugins and the gateway's own sensor remain outside that result.

Three Ameba builds have a recovered TemSen numeric path consistent with offset 40, but then reach an unavailable ROM `strcmp(NULL)` tail before a completed response is established: embedded RT13 V1.04/V1.10 and WB05MAJD V1.03. They remain **unresolved**, not independently usable fixed-offset evidence. The code-wide `362001065279` fallback nevertheless covers RT13 V1.04/V1.10; this does not relabel those binaries confirmed. Likewise, a shared TemSen cache or a successful seeded getter replay cannot establish the units of an untraced sensor producer.

## Wi-Fi protocol indicators

The follow-up covered all **67 schema-supported payloads**: 46 Realtek/Ameba, 17 Xtensa, G06, Xradio, and two route-scoped gateway containers. It also compared all 12 confirmed offset-0 builds and five runtime-dependent builds: **84 distinct source SHA-256 values** in total. G06's proved endpoint is cellular/cloud, not Wi-Fi; the containers prove selected appliance plugins, not a gateway-wide LAN dispatcher.

**No safe status/property query advertising a TemSen offset or valid physical Celsius range was established in these inspected paths.** Ordinary replies carry names in `cols` and numbers in `dat`; that shape is shared by raw-forwarding and already-normalized implementations. Local UART extraction descriptors are not remotely advertised temperature schemas.

| Candidate signal | Recovered meaning / limitation |
| --- | --- |
| `TemUn`, `TemRec` | Separate control-byte bits 7 and 6. With the incoming sensor byte held fixed, changing these bits does not change the traced Wi-Fi-side TemSen acquisition/serialization. This does not prove how a physical mainboard might change future bytes after a display-unit change. |
| `BlkTemCom` | A boolean control flag in the inspected Xtensa UART schema, not a numeric 40-degree encoding selector. Mainboard-side compensation remains possible. |
| `HeatSvStTemMax`, `CoolSvStTemMin`, `HumiSvStTemMin` | Control/setpoint limits, not TemSen measurement bounds. Target temperature is not an ambient calibration reference. |
| Byte width / descriptor mask `255` | The unsigned UART carrier can represent 0…255; this is not a valid Celsius range, a sentinel specification, or evidence that every represented value is a real measurement. |
| MID, CPU/module name, primary HID/version | Identity or routing evidence, not a units flag. Exact inspected builds/routes can support a lookup; broad groups cannot replace the recovered conversion path. `BtHid` and `InHid` are not primary `hid`. |
| OTA `commProtVer` | Catalog metadata, not an established LAN offset attribute. The saved `V3.0.0` label occurs for normalized [W04N V17](docs/HIDS.md#sha-b0b7f5d428858ca5e5ad29a8afcfaa0da4063fe36e98fae7c447047163112727) and raw-forwarding [W04H V3.55](docs/HIDS.md#sha-17b1c7812a3f98fa1e800e786b37606d842ebbf0b38a25c0ec5c173fcaaa9f2e) / [WB04A V1.49](docs/HIDS.md#sha-0d5ee6fda1678084e1fa29e6267941afae304090b5b97f7b4264f9cee7fd461b). |

A concrete LAN chain is W04H V3.55: UDP7000 receive `0x08053440` → envelope parser `0x080525d0` → plaintext dispatcher `0x08052234` → status `0x08052ff0` → reply selector `0x08052088(3)` → builder `0x08052914(4)`. Native inner-request replay emitted `cols=["TemSen"], dat=[63]`; separate unit-property requests emitted their bits, not a range descriptor. Socket/envelope linkage is static evidence; crypto/JSON boundaries were hooked, not a complete encrypted network exchange.

Across Realtek/Ameba, all 35 registered request tables and 11 inline dispatch chains were resolved. Candidate range/unit request types or property names produced no descriptor. Thirteen unsupported-property probes stopped at a real NULL table slot before an unavailable ROM comparison; complete omission is not claimed for those cases. An isolated G06 unknown-name probe also reached a NULL comparison. **Do not probe unknown names or write temperature/control fields on live devices to infer calibration.** `dump`, reporting controls and configuration operations are not necessarily read-only capability queries.

### Selector changes do not label cached samples

The five runtime getters use internal `startcloud_id == 9` (GREE), not MID, `TemUn`, outer `cid`/`tcid`, or a public range field. No safe observational LAN getter for that selector was established. In three builds, ordinary LAN reception and a Gree status snapshot actually **persist selector 9**:

| Exact build / SHA | Receive slice → parser | Registry wrapper → installed callback → snapshot |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [BL3332_SN V13](docs/HIDS.md#sha-c38c120c2f125dfc5e90917265860d0efb0bfa8089b8586fa2466b22fe17063b) | `0x08033996 → 0x08030250` | `0x0802e538 → 0x08025e7c → 0x08025c54` |
| [BL3332_TMAC V14](docs/HIDS.md#sha-7f8a0a059c8a434ac5caef6b42b78dcfdb112939d5d7027a7a0b743da50bb065) | `0x08033fae → 0x080307b8` | `0x0802f0a0 → 0x08027c3c → 0x080278b4` |
| [W04N V19](docs/HIDS.md#sha-0b01a0d0fd25651b81bf435e46428987a3b43b905b2ee6bdc0714c6030452d92) | `0x08032fb6 → 0x0802f8c8` | `0x0802dc50 → 0x08025a04 → 0x080257dc` |

This does **not** establish unconditional LAN offset 40. The synchronous status callback serializes cached fields without calling the temperature getter. Native sequential replay first obtained **25** or **65** from the actual getter with internal sample 65 under selector 0 or 9, then seeded that result into the real 130-field compiled snapshot schema. Entering the actual registered callback with selector 0 persisted 9, but still emitted the seeded **25** or **65**; the temperature getter was never invoked during the read. The cache refreshers copy incoming `cols/dat` or `opt/p` integers unchanged, separately from the status read.

This proves a handler-state/cache boundary, not an observed physical-device transition: boot/reconfiguration sequences producing those cache states and the complete encrypted packet path were not replayed. JD V18 and JDNEW V12 lack the corresponding receive/snapshot selector writes in the inspected call graph. All five builds remain runtime-dependent; ingress normalization alone is not grounds for a new fixed trait.

### A narrower, build-specific sample discriminator

For genuine values produced by **these five exact runtime getters only**—SN V13, JD V18, TMAC V14, JDNEW V12 and W04N V19—the selector-9 output support is `{0} ∪ [40,90]`; other selectors produce `[0,50]`.

| Returned TemSen | What these getter bounds establish |
| --- | --- |
| 1…39 | This sample uses offset 0. |
| 51…90 | This sample uses offset 40. |
| 40…50 | Both encodings remain possible; 45 could represent 45°C or 5°C. |
| 0 | Ambiguous/collapsed-low input; never infer −40°C from it. |
| Below 0 or above 90 | Outside these getters' output support; not evidence of another encoding. |

This is a **sample-encoding discriminator**, not proof of the current selector or a device-lifetime invariant. It does not require a setpoint, assumed room temperature, repeated polls, or cross-sensor history. Do not extend it to other firmware, command echoes, unsupported fields, or untraced cache producers. In particular, the 1…39 rule is **not** a replacement for the generic detection policy below: raw-forwarding UART builds can forward those same byte values.

### Follow-up verification scope

The source hashes were checked per slice. Realtek/Ameba native execution passed 2,024 acquisition/unit/boundary cases, 140 request-dispatch probes, 354 status/property probes (including the bounded NULL-slot cases), 264 full-status cases and three actual inner LAN request/reply cases. Non-Realtek schema paths passed 36 native MCU cases, 306 byte-matched Xtensa serializer cases and 36 native routed-plugin cases. The normalized/runtime comparison passed 1,470 native value assertions across 15 ARM builds, 512 byte-matched inputs across two Xtensa builds, 15 receive-boundary cases and six registered-callback snapshot cases, plus unit/mode checks.

These are bounded native or exact-byte translated checks with documented API/ROM hooks, not firmware boots or physical Celsius calibration. The analysis reports, per-SHA addresses, fixtures and outputs are retained locally under `/tmp/gree-expanded-survey/wifi-followup/{realtek,xtensa-other,selector}/`. Evidence classes remain unchanged; the production registry applies the explicitly qualified code-wide policy above.

## Automatic detection and precedence

For unknown or unflagged profiles, precedence is **learned offset → profile offset → first guess → unavailable**. For re-learning profiles, precedence is **latest learned offset → retained first guess → profile offset → unavailable**. A guess obtained before the HID arrives therefore survives a runtime bootstrap default. Each device caches its last HID's matched profile, including no match, and refreshes it on the next lookup after the HID changes. This also covers direct assignment to `Device.hid`. A HID-only update may change how a cached sample is displayed, but never learns or resets inference; incoming samples still drive temperature re-learning independently of the profile cache.

### Unknown and unflagged firmware

Generic learning assumes indoor temperatures in the inclusive **−20°C…50°C** envelope. For a raw integer `r`, the candidates are `r` and `r − 40`. Only incoming `TemSen` samples supply evidence:

| Raw `TemSen` | Action before a permanent lock |
| --- | --- |
| −20…19, excluding zero | Learn offset 0 permanently |
| 20…50 | Keep the first guess: 0 below 40, otherwise 40 |
| 51…90 | Learn offset 40 permanently |
| Zero, noninteger, or outside −20…90 | Do not learn or change the guess |

The first ambiguous guess is retained even while an unflagged trait supplies the displayed offset. A later decisive sample overrides both the guess and trait, regardless of metadata arrival order. While the profile remains unknown or unflagged, that learned offset never changes, including after contradictory samples or reconnect. A newly constructed `Device` starts independently. Neither repeated ambiguous readings nor metadata changes confirm or reset a guess.

Missing, noninteger (including boolean), and out-of-union raw samples return `None`, never the target setpoint, without changing offset state. Zero provides no evidence: with an independently selected offset 0 it displays 0°C; with offset 40 or no selected offset it is unavailable. Partial packets preserve the cached sensor; explicit `TemSen=None` makes it unavailable. Local property mutations bypass learning.

The generic envelope is an inference assumption and a gross raw-value guard, not a decoded-temperature clamp. On unflagged devices, learned0 followed by raw65 displays 65°C; learned40 followed by raw19 displays −21°C. Unit conversion follows offset correction; an unrepresentable Fahrenheit conversion returns `None` without changing inference.

An extreme actual temperature can cause an incorrect first permanent lock. The envelope is not universal hardware calibration, and code-only defaults generalize beyond inspected builds. No manual reset, persistence, voting, or historical-range resolver is provided.

### Continuous re-learning families

The five flagged code families apply the inspected runtime getter bounds to **every actual incoming TemSen**, even after learning. This is deliberately code-wide despite version-specific evidence; it is not extended to unknown or unflagged raw-forwarding firmware.

| Incoming `TemSen` | Inference action |
| --- | --- |
| Integer 1…39 | Replace learned offset with 0 |
| Integer 51…90 | Replace learned offset with 40 |
| Integer 40…50 | Retain learned offset; without learning or an earlier guess, set first guess to 40 |
| Zero | Do not learn or change the guess |
| None, boolean, other noninteger, negative, or above 90 | Do not mutate either inference field |

Flagged raw values outside integer 0…90 are unavailable. Zero uses the shared display rule above. Ambiguity never reverses the latest decisive inference, and no guess is created or changed after learning. For example, W04N V19 samples `65,25,45,15,55` display `25,25,45,15,15`°C; samples `45,25,45,65,45` display `5,25,45,25,5`°C.

HID-only updates, unrelated partial packets, getters and local property mutations do not learn. The newly matched flag governs the next actual sensor sample, without resetting retained state. Unknown15 → HID-only W04N → incoming55 displays15 throughout; unknown25 → HID-only W04N → incoming45 → incoming65 displays25,25,45,25. Switching back to an unflagged profile freezes the latest learned offset under the one-time policy.

An ambiguous estimate can be wrong by **40°C** until decisive evidence arrives; repeated polls do not make it certain. A metadata change can leave old inference interpreting a cached sample incorrectly until new evidence arrives. The selector/cache replay does not prove physical transition frequency or freshness. Re-learning adapts only when a decisive sample is observed; it is not a guarantee that the cached sample reflects the current selector.

## Investigation provenance and limits

The investigation unpacked supported image/container formats, inspected native code and application schemas, and used bounded native ARM/RISC-V execution with API-edge hooks or byte-matched Xtensa instruction replay where available. Each row records its actual proof and remaining blockers; inspection does not imply that every image was fully decompiled or that its decoder completed successfully. Checks were not firmware boots, full-device emulation, or hardware calibration. Schema-supported results remain conditional on the attached mainboard using the documented UART encoding. Alternate fields such as `EnvironmentTem`, `TemBody`, `TemSor`, and `EnvTem` have separate transports and are not interchangeable with `TemSen`.

The original W04N V7 image was not in this inventory. Its behavior remains unverified: a complete HID reporting `362001064973` receives the code-wide re-learning policy and provisional fallback, not a verified fixed W04N encoding. No firmware binaries, private captures, or temporary analysis files are required at runtime.
